--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion uses a 2-state FSM:
- **State 0**: GoToColor (blue, clr=3) → moves toward blue walls (the rewarded upper strip)
- **State 1**: Attraction → clusters with neighbors
- Transition 0→1: gray floor (p=0.1378) — when crossing gray, switch to attraction
- Transition 1→0: fixed probability (p=0.2915) — occasionally re-explore

This makes sense: go toward blue walls, when crossing gray (middle) start attracting, occasionally re-check direction. Score 11415 is quite good.

**Key insight**: The color for blue walls is clr=3 (blue=3 in {1=red,2=green,3=blue,4=yellow,5=magenta,6=cyan}).

**Strategy**:
1. **Elitism**: Copy champion exactly
2. **Exploitation**: Tweak champion parameters slightly
3. **Exploration**: Try different topologies - e.g., 3-state machines that add a "stay on black" state, or use GoAwayColor from green (clr=2) instead

For the black floor detection: once on black in the upper strip, robots should stop or attract strongly. The problem is distinguishing upper black from lower black - only color perception can do that.

**New ideas**:
- 3-state: GoToBlue → on black + see blue → Stop/Attract; fallback to GoToBlue
- 2-state: GoToBlue with black floor trigger to Attraction (instead of gray)
- GoAwayColor from green (clr=2) as alternative to GoToColor blue
- Combine both: go toward blue AND away from green

Let me design 14 machines:

1. **Champion** (exact copy)
2. **Exploit**: p0x0=0.08 (lower gray prob), att higher
3. **Exploit**: p1x0=0.15 (less random re-explore)
4. **Exploit**: clr0=3 blue, transition on black floor instead of gray
5. **Exploit**: 3-state with stop on black+blue
6. **Explore**: GoAwayColor green (clr=2) + attraction
7. **Explore**: 3-state GoToBlue → black → stop, with color check
8. **Explore**: GoToBlue with multiple transitions
9. **Exploit**: att gain higher
10. **Explore**: GoAwayGreen + black floor stop
11. **Exploit**: slightly different probabilities
12. **Explore**: 3-state machine
13. **Explore**: 4-state with blue detection
14. **Exploit**: different combination

## Reasoning

The champion (score 8148) is a 3-state FSM:
- **State 0**: GoToBlue (clr=3) → on black floor (p=0.65) → go to State 1
- **State 1**: Attraction (att=3) → random prob (p=0.3) → back to State 0; gray floor (p=0.8) → back to State 1 (self-loop... wait, n1x1 goes to destination 1 which with 3 states from state 1, others are [0,2], so D=1 means state 2)
- **State 2**: GoToBlue (clr=3) → gray floor (p=0.2) → State 1

So the logic is: GoToBlue → hit black → attract → if gray (leaving black) go back to GoToBlue; also from attract, occasionally re-explore. State 2 is another GoToBlue that feeds back to attraction on gray detection.

The 2nd best (8493) uses GoToBlue + see green → GoAwayGreen pattern, which also works well.

**Exploitation strategy**:
1. Copy champion exactly
2. Tweak champion: adjust black floor probability (0.65 → 0.55, 0.75), attraction gain, re-explore probability
3. Tweak champion: add a second transition from state 0 (gray floor or see blue)
4. Tweak champion: state 1 add color detection
5. Explore variants of the 3-state topology with different parameters

**Key observations**:
- Black floor detection is the trigger to switch from moving to attracting (good - confirms upper strip arrival)
- State 2 (another GoToBlue) handles robots that drift back to gray and need to return
- The gray floor transition from state 1 (p=0.8) keeps robots from staying attracted when they drift to middle

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion (8148) and top performers are converging on the same 3-state topology:
- **State 0**: GoToBlue (clr=3) → black floor (p≈0.55-0.75) → State 1
- **State 1**: Attraction (att≈3-4) → random (p≈0.15-0.3) → State 0; gray floor (p≈0.75-0.9) → State 2
- **State 2**: GoToBlue (clr=3) → gray floor (p≈0.1-0.25) → State 1

Key parameters to tune:
- Black floor prob in state 0: 0.55-0.75 range seems good
- Attraction gain: 3-4 range
- Random re-explore prob in state 1: lower seems better (0.15-0.25)
- Gray floor prob in state 1: higher seems better (0.8-0.9)
- Gray floor prob in state 2: lower seems better (0.1-0.25)

The 4th best adds a GoAwayGreen state (state 3) triggered by seeing green (clr=2), which adds robustness.

**Plan**:
1. Champion copy (elitism)
2-6. Fine exploitation of champion topology with parameter grid around the sweet spots
7-8. Exploit the 4-state winner with GoAwayGreen
9-11. Try variations with additional transitions (color perception for blue in state 1)
12-14. Exploration: different black floor probs, different att gains, add a stop state

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion (8148) persists and the top performers cluster tightly around it. Key observations:

1. **Champion topology is robust**: 3-state GoToBlue→Attract→GoToBlue with black floor trigger and gray floor return
2. **Best of round 4** is a 4-state variant that adds a GoAwayGreen fallback (score 8212) - this is interesting but didn't beat champion
3. **Parameter sweet spots emerging**:
   - State 0 black floor prob: 0.5-0.75 (wider range still good)
   - State 1 att gain: 2.5-4.0 (wider range)
   - State 1 random re-explore: 0.15-0.3
   - State 1 gray floor prob: 0.8-0.9
   - State 2 gray floor prob: 0.1-0.25
4. **Adding see-green transition in state 1** (c1x2=7, l1x2=2) with destination state 3 (GoAwayGreen) helped (score 8212)

**Strategy for round 5**:
- Copy champion (elitism)
- Exploit: fine-tune champion params more carefully - try att=3.2, p0=0.68, etc.
- Exploit: explore the 4-state winner more carefully with different GoAwayGreen params
- Explore: try combining see-blue trigger in state 2 to return to attract faster
- Explore: try a version where state 1 also checks black floor to stay attracted

The key insight I haven't tried: in state 1 (attraction), add a transition back to state 1 itself... wait, can't self-loop. But I can add a transition to state 2 on seeing green (escape wrong area), and have state 2 go back to state 1 on black floor (correct area detected again).

Also: the 4-state winner sends green-perceivers from state 1 to state 2 (another GoToBlue), and has state 3 (GoAwayGreen) as a separate recovery state. Let me try more variants of that.

The new champion (8135) differs from the previous one by adding a 3rd transition in state 1: "see blue (clr=3) → go back to state 0" with p=0.8. This makes sense - if a robot in the attraction state sees blue walls, it confirms it's near the target and can stay, or if it drifted away it can re-navigate. The LED color in state 1 is now magenta (cle1=5) instead of yellow.

**Key findings**:
1. The 3-transition state 1 with "see blue → return to GoToBlue" added value
2. State 2 with "see green → return to state 0" also helped (score 8183)
3. The core structure is very robust: GoToBlue(black→attract) ↔ Attract ↔ GoToBlue(gray→attract)

**Exploitation plan**:
- Vary the blue-perception probability in the new champion (0.8 → 0.7, 0.9)
- Vary state 1 random prob (0.3 → 0.25, 0.35)
- Vary state 1 gray floor prob (0.8 → 0.75, 0.85)
- Combine see-blue in state 1 AND see-green in state 2
- Try 4-state with both green escape and blue perception
- Try state 2 with see-green return to state 0

**Exploration**:
- Try att gain 3.5 with the new 3-transition topology
- Try different black floor prob in state 0 (0.6, 0.7)
- Try adding a 4th transition for neighbor count in state 1

## Reasoning

The new champion (7893) is a 4-state FSM:
- **State 0**: GoToBlue → black floor (p=0.65) → State 1
- **State 1**: Attraction (att=3) → random (p=0.28) → State 0; gray floor (p=0.82) → State 2; see blue (p=0.8) → State 0
- **State 2**: GoToBlue → gray floor (p=0.18) → State 1; see green (p=0.75) → State 3 (GoAwayGreen)
- **State 3**: GoAwayGreen → black floor (p=0.65) → State 0

This is elegant: GoToBlue is the main drive, attraction when on black, escape from green walls via GoAwayGreen, return to blue when drifting to gray.

**Key insights**:
1. The 4-state with GoAwayGreen fallback beat the 3-state versions
2. The 3-state with 4 transitions in state 1 (seeing both blue and green) scored 7947 - very close
3. State 2 seeing green and routing to GoAwayGreen is a good escape mechanism

**Exploitation plan**:
- Champion copy (elitism)
- Tweak champion: vary p0x0 (0.6, 0.7), att gain (3.2, 3.5), random prob (0.25, 0.3), gray prob (0.78, 0.85), blue-see prob (0.75, 0.85), state2 green prob (0.7, 0.8), state3 black prob (0.6, 0.7)
- Exploit 2nd best (3-state with 4 transitions in state 1): vary params
- Try 4-state where state 3 GoAwayGreen also feeds back to state 1 on black

**Exploration**:
- Try state 1 with 4 transitions including see-green escape
- Try state 3 with multiple transitions

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The new champion (7658) adds a second transition in state 0: "black floor (p=0.92) → state 1" in addition to the first "black floor (p=0.65) → state 1". This is interesting - having two black floor transitions in the same state essentially gives a higher effective black-floor detection probability (combined ~0.97).

**Key observations from round 8**:
1. The champion structure is now very stable: 4-state with double-black-floor in state 0
2. Scores 2nd and 3rd (7664) are essentially the same machine without the double-transition in state 0 - confirming the double-transition helps
3. The tight cluster (7658-7684) suggests we're near a local optimum
4. Worst performers are mostly minor variations that didn't help much

**Fine-tuning targets**:
- State 0: p0x0=0.65, p0x1=0.92 → try (0.6/0.9), (0.7/0.95), (0.65/0.88)
- State 1: att=3, random=0.28, gray=0.82, see-blue=0.8 → try att=3.5, vary probs slightly
- State 2: gray=0.2, see-green=0.72 → try 0.22/0.70, 0.18/0.75
- State 3: black=0.65, see-blue=0.85 → try 0.67/0.87, 0.62/0.88

**Exploration**: Try combining double-black in state 0 with att=3.2 or 3.5, try state 3 with 3 transitions.

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
OBJECTIVE. At every simulation step the loop function adds +1 to the score for each robot that is NOT inside the target region. The score is MINIMISED, so a lower score means the swarm reached the region earlier and more robots stayed in it.

THE TARGET REGION is the upper strip of the arena. It is NOT identified by a unique floor colour: the floor is BLACK in BOTH the upper and the lower strip, and GRAY in between. Only the UPPER black strip is rewarded. Sitting on the lower black strip scores exactly as badly as standing in the middle of the arena.

HOW TO TELL THE TWO STRIPS APART. At the start of the run the arena walls light up: the two walls bordering the REWARDED upper strip are BLUE, the two walls bordering the penalised lower strip are GREEN. Colour is the only reliable cue.

CONSEQUENCE FOR THE FSM. A black-floor condition is useful to detect that a strip has been reached, but on its own it sends half the swarm to the wrong strip. It must be combined with colour-driven motion - move toward BLUE, or move away from GREEN - to reach the correct one. Once inside, robots should stay rather than keep exploring, since every step spent outside costs a point. Clustering tightly is NOT rewarded: only being inside counts.

# 2. THE ARENA FOR THIS RUN
The arena is a regular polygon with 6 walls, inner radius 0.81 m. 29 e-puck robots start scattered uniformly in a square of half-width 0.65 m around the centre, with random orientations. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.32 m: neighbour-count conditions only see robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).
For reference, the published setup uses 20 robots and a range of 0.50 m, so thresholds tuned for that setup may need adjusting here.

# 3. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 4. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 5. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 6. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
